
===== AGENTS.md =====
    1 # V3 — agent instructions
    2 
    3 **ADOPTED 2026-09-19. These files are the active repository instructions. They
    4 authorise only the scope recorded in the owner-direction register below; they do
    5 not authorise implementation, runs, or acceptance beyond it.**
    6 
    7 ## Purpose and authority
    8 
    9 Build a new engine in a separate repository for a small living world where
   10 survival, experience, and relationships produce lasting consequences. Begin with
   11 six people; design and benchmark for 50. Larger populations are a later objective.
   12 
   13 Read three governing documents:
   14 
   15 1. `AGENTS.md`: authority, scope, progression, and reporting.
   16 2. [DOCTRINE.md](DOCTRINE.md): mandatory correctness and evidence principles.
   17 3. [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md): ordered objectives, deliverables, and exit conditions.
   18 
   19 The roadmap cannot override doctrine. Evidence records implement these rules;
   20 they cannot amend them. Explicit owner directions control changes to purpose,
   21 scope, or the governing documents. Adopt the three files together in one commit.
   22 Record later amendments in the dated owner-direction register below; amendment
   23 commits cite the entry and retain the preceding version.
   24 
   25 The old project is reference material only. Preserve its checkout, evidence, and
   26 pins. Do not import its instruction hierarchy, target its trajectories, resume
   27 its feature train, or copy domains without a current requirement and review.
   28 
   29 ## Session entry and working authority
   30 
   31 Read the three governing files and the active stage record. Check the actual code
   32 revision, outstanding findings, review status, and budget remaining. Briefly state
   33 the active objective and next permitted action, then continue it. Do not infer a
   34 pass from a stage name, a prior summary, or an implemented mechanism.
   35 
   36 Stages 1–5 define the development scope. Work on one active stage and one concrete
   37 objective. Routine design, implementation, focused tests, and declared exploration
   38 within that scope need no repeated owner approval. Stage 6 requires a newly chosen
   39 owner objective. No merge, publication, destructive operation, or alteration of
   40 the reference project is implied by stage progression.
   41 
   42 Use `evidence/stage-01/RECORD.md` through `evidence/stage-06/RECORD.md` for the
   43 consolidated stage records. Track each record and its configuration from its first
   44 commit. Link raw artifacts by immutable identity and accessible location; do not
   45 silently ignore or discard evidence. Only create records for stages actually opened.
   46 
   47 Create the short active-stage card when work starts. Complete its run-specific
   48 definitions, limits, and criteria before any exploration, confirmation, or benchmark
   49 run. Routine implementation and focused defect tests need no separate card or
   50 approval. Append attempts and results beneath the card, preserving failed versions.
   51 
   52 Adoption accepts the roadmap's proposed budget ceilings and coverage floors.
   53 Agents declare exact values and allowed variations within that envelope before
   54 use. Changing a candidate within the declared exploration levers consumes the same
   55 budget. A confirmation attempt stays frozen through its recorded result. Changing
   56 a counting predicate, required confirmation criterion, allowed levers, or budget
   57 ceiling requires a recorded owner direction; it creates a new attempt, not a
   58 retrospective pass or an automatic budget reset. Routine invariant repairs remain
   59 authorised.
   60 
   61 ## Exit review
   62 
   63 A stage advances only when its stated exit conditions are satisfied and a separate
   64 reviewer using a different model has examined the evidence. Supply one packet:
   65 stage record and original criteria; source/configuration identities; instrument
   66 checks; raw evidence and reproduction commands; failed attempts; claimed conclusion.
   67 Give the reviewer access to the pinned source and full supporting artifacts.
   68 
   69 The reviewer runs the applicable reference test suite and independently reproduces
   70 at least one central quantitative claim from the sealed evidence or a declared
   71 rerun. Record reviewer/model identity, claims rerun versus read, discrepancies,
   72 and a reasoned PASS, FAIL, or UNKNOWN verdict against each essential exit condition.
   73 An inaccessible artifact or summary-only review cannot support PASS. Review work
   74 uses reserved run capacity; it does not renew the budget.
   75 
   76 If review is unavailable or inconclusive, mark progression pending. Evidence
   77 packaging, focused tests, and defect repair within the active scope may continue;
   78 next-stage implementation and runs may not. Do not substitute self-review or
   79 presume a pass. The owner may supply the independent reviewer; an agent must not
   80 invent one or silently waive the requirement.
   81 Review does not replace tests or make a deficient measurement trustworthy.
   82 
   83 Exhausted budgets or unresolved prerequisites stop the affected stage. Renaming
   84 an attempt, changing versions, or moving it to another stage does not reset its
   85 budget. Present the missing link and bounded alternatives to the owner.
   86 
   87 Repair a later-found kernel defect within the active stage and budget. Mark affected
   88 earlier claims invalid or pending revalidation, preserve the original results, and
   89 rerun the relevant reference checks. Revalidate affected dependencies before using
   90 them again; do not restart unaffected stages or preserve a bug behind a flag.
   91 Expected-output changes require an explained, independently reviewed replacement,
   92 with the old result retained. Invariant assertions are not weakened.
   93 
   94 ## Reporting
   95 
   96 State what changed, what was measured, which checks passed, unresolved limitations,
   97 review status, and rollback. Separate reported history from current measurements,
   98 implementation from demonstrated behaviour, and demonstration from owner acceptance.
   99 
  100 **Reproducibility alone does not establish correctness or causation.**
  101 
  102 ## Owner-direction register
  103 
  104 This is the single location for dated direction entries: ID, exact owner direction,
  105 affected scope or rule, reason, and linked revision. Evidence records cite entries
  106 rather than creating another source of authority. A proposed entry is not an owner
  107 decision.
  108

  357    from that file, never from live state. Sealing, replay and recovery are
  358    deferred. This is checkpoint 1.

  360    the run output so drift is visible.

  362    renewable food source, hunger (checkpoint 2, a map over time); then
  363    perception radius and bounded views (checkpoint 3). The Stage 2
  364    opportunity-counting instrument and confirmation floors are not opened by
  365    this direction.

  367    reference tests, one record file per run. No frozen hashes, no evidence
  368    contracts, no independent acceptance until the owner names a result worth
  369    ratifying.

  373 1b are reviewed and correct, and the next risk is building a world nobody can

  376 Checkpoint output is exploration, not evidence: anything worth keeping is
  377 re-run under the ratification rules before it is cited as a result.

  466 ### OD-010 - 2026-09-21 - Open exploration leg 5: inter-agent state, visual checkpoint B
  467 
  468 **Exact owner direction.** Recorded from the owner's message of 2026-09-21
  469 ("ok") accepting the drafted declaration below in full.
  470 
  471 **Behaviour targeted.** A hungry person walking to the source who can see it
  472 crowded holds back instead of stepping on, unless in emergency. Whether they
  473 hold back depends on a trait, so people with the same view act differently.
  474 No social action: nobody requests, gives, follows or avoids a particular
  475 person. The trait is a model assumption (DOCTRINE 8), declared and recorded,
  476 not a finding.
  477 
  478 **Canonical state it changes.** The overlay gains one immutable per-person
  479 trait assigned at genesis: `yield_at`, an integer drawn by the seeded genesis
  480 generator from a declared set (default {1, 2, 3}), written to the run header.
  481 Nothing else in canonical state changes. The kernel is untouched. No new
  482 stored population state: crowd counts are derived from positions already
  483 recorded.
  484 
  485 **Rule (exact).** At tick start, a person who is hungry, not in emergency,
  486 not on the source, and who can see the source cell, counts the living people
  487 standing on it. If that count is >= their `yield_at` and the observed source
  488 stock is < that count, the person's decision is `yield`: stay where they are
  489 this tick. Otherwise the existing rule applies unchanged. Emergency (hunger
  490 >= emergency_at) never yields. Hunger keeps rising while yielding (DOCTRINE
  491 1). Dead people are not counted and, as already implemented, not seen; that
  492 becomes a declared line in the config description.
  493 
  494 **What becomes visible (checkpoint B).** On the map: people holding position
  495 at the rim of the source with their trait shown; a `yield` decision with its
  496 reason (for example "saw 3 on source, stock 2, yield_at 2"). Over time: crowd
  497 on the source cell per tick, yield events, and deaths and emergency
  498 person-ticks broken down by trait value.
  499 
  500 **Control.** `--yield off` sets every `yield_at` beyond the actor count so no
  501 one ever yields; that run must match `9d354414` decisions byte-for-byte
  502 (extending the existing fixture test). The checkpoint reports seed 7 and two
  503 more seeds ON and OFF side by side: survivors, deaths, emergency
  504 person-ticks, denied claims. This is a picture of a difference, not a claim
  505 about it.
  506 
  507 **Tests.** OFF byte-identity against the HEAD fixture; yield occurs only when
  508 the recorded observation satisfies the rule, checked from the run file;
  509 emergency never yields; trait assignment is seeded, identity-neutral, from
  510 the declared set, and present in the header; determinism; tamper on the trait
  511 block fails verification; ownership unchanged (kernel imports nothing from
  512 world).
  513 
  514 **Stop condition.** The leg closes when checkpoint B renders from the run
  515 file and the seed-7 ON run contains at least one yield event with its ON/OFF
  516 comparison recorded in evidence/stage-01/RECORD.md. It halts and reports if
  517 implementing it would need a social action, a retained fact or observation
  518 age, or any kernel change.
  519 
  520 **Held constant.** The claim-then-eat latency rule (two deaths at the source
  521 holding food, checkpoint 2) stays as it is for this leg so the ON/OFF
  522 comparison is not confounded; it is a separate owner decision after
  523 checkpoint B.
  524 
  525 **Standing.** Exploration lane only under OD-009 Revision 3; no ratification,
  526 no Stage exit; one active leg. Local commit, no push. Alternatives set aside
  527 at drafting: a trait that does not use perception (speed, appetite)
  528 differentiates people but does not make them notice each other; moving
  529 toward a seen food-holder makes them notice each other but is a request-food
  530 precursor, which is Stage 3 and parked.
  531 
  532 **Reason.** Leg 5 is the next undelivered item in the Revision 3 order and
  533 requires an owner direction with a pre-code declaration. Crowd-yielding uses
  534 the perception delivered at checkpoint 3 without opening a social action, a
  535 memory, or a kernel change.

  537 **Linked revision.** Recorded on `codex/kernel-first-slice` after
  538 `aef393fd`. The recording commit's hash cannot appear inside this entry.

  540 ### OD-011 - 2026-09-21 - Open exploration leg 6: scored actions, checkpoint C
  541 
  542 **Exact owner direction.** "next stage", followed by "your best decision"
  543 when asked whether to score a person's actions or competing food claimants.
  544 This opens the next exploration leg, not a roadmap Stage exit. The concrete
  545 choice and modelling declarations below are the agent's implementation design
  546 under that delegation, not a quotation or independent owner acceptance.
  547 
  548 **Behaviour targeted.** Score existing eligible actions using the person's
  549 tick-start observation. When yielding is eligible, increasing hunger can
  550 outweigh crowd pressure before emergency. Food allocation still uses the
  551 existing kernel rotation; action scores confer no priority over other people.
  552 
  553 **Pre-code declaration.** In opt-in `--scoring on`, compare integer pairs
  554 `(tier, pressure)` lexicographically, greatest first: EAT `(2,0)`;
  555 CLAIM/WAIT `(1,0)`; GO `(0,2*(hunger-hungry_at)+1)`;
  556 YIELD `(0,2*(seen_crowd-yield_at+1))`; HOME/REST `(0,0)`.
  557 Eligibility is unchanged, including emergency exclusion of YIELD. EAT always
  558 wins when eligible. GO/YIELD pressures have opposite parity, so these
  559 competing actions cannot tie; CLAIM/WAIT and HOME/REST are mutually exclusive.
  560 No new action tie policy is needed. Equivalent food claims continue to resolve
  561 by the sorted tick-start kernel roster rotated by `tick mod actor_count`,
  562 including ledger actors who have died; dead people make no decisions or claims.
  563 Do not substitute claimant-count or living-count rotation. These scores are
  564 authored assumptions, not findings or a new allocation policy.
  565 
  566 **Canonical state.** No new retained state, trait or kernel schema. Changed
  567 choices may change ordinary positions, hunger, food and deaths. Scored decisions
  568 record their actual eligible score pairs at selection time in the existing
  569 decision block. `process.py` and the kernel are unchanged. The header declares
  570 the scoring mode and formula. Default/OFF retains the leg-5 decisions exactly.
  571 
  572 **Visible checkpoint.** Show each eligible action's native score and selected
  573 action; show the kernel's recorded rotation, claim requests, accepted/denied
  574 outcomes and effects. Provide a jump to a contested claim and a scored
  575 GO-versus-YIELD choice. Render HTML and text from saved run files only.
  576 
  577 **Control and budget.** Fixed leg-5 seed-7 world, 300 ticks, yield ON: scoring
  578 ON twice and OFF once, at most three whole-world executions for this leg.
  579 No tuning, seed search, horizon extension or capacity runs. OFF decisions and
  580 physical trajectory must match the existing leg-5 run. Keep prior artifacts.
  581 Focused tests are not whole-world exploration slots; no stage budget is reset.
  582 
  583 **Tests and stop.** Existing reference suite unchanged; focused checks for
  584 score crossover, EAT/emergency safety, observation bounds, input-order
  585 independence, native score tampering, unchanged atomic settlement, and exact
  586 rotation including inactive actors. Stop when the saved seed-7 ON file shows
  587 a GO/YIELD scored choice, contains contested food claims resolved by recorded
  588 rotation, renders checkpoint C, and the fixed comparison/determinism result
  589 is recorded. A missing demonstration is reported, not tuned into a pass.
  590 
  591 **Standing.** Local only, exploration only. Claim-then-eat latency and world
  592 levers stay fixed. No social action, memory, orchestrator, ratification or
  593 Stage exit. Checkpoint C acceptance remains the owner's; leg 7 is not opened.
  594 Base revision: `768a454951bb2ec82ceb224a9065ecb785b31516`.

  596 #### OD-011 revised implementation direction — 2026-09-22, before code
  597 
  598 The owner's detailed leg-6 instruction revises the declaration above. The
  599 preceding declaration remains history; its local-only standing is superseded
  600 only by the conditional push authority below. The formula remains the previous
  601 agent's delegated design, not a verbatim owner specification or accepted finding.
  602 
  603 **Scale and crossover.** One additional hunger point and one additional observed
  604 person each add two pressure units. This equal weighting is a modelling
  605 assumption, not an empirically established scale. For simultaneously eligible
  606 GO and YIELD, GO wins exactly when
  607 `hunger >= hungry_at + seen_crowd - yield_at + 1`; at `hungry_at = 5`,
  608 `hunger >= seen_crowd - yield_at + 6`. Record both forms in the run header and
  609 stage record. Test immediately below and at the boundary, and directly assert
  610 GO odd, YIELD even and never equal across the eligible domain. This establishes
  611 a possible crossover, not occurrence in the fixed run, better survival or fewer
  612 whole-run yields. A yield episode can shorten under fixed conditions; trajectory
  613 feedback may change total yields in either direction.
  614 
  615 **Preserved boundaries.** Scores use only bounded tick-start observations and
  616 declared configuration. The one live selector records actual eligible pairs in
  617 the existing decision block. Default/OFF keeps exact leg-5 decision shape and
  618 values. Personal scores never rank food claims. The unchanged kernel rotates
  619 the sorted full tick-start roster, including inactive ledger actors, by
  620 `tick mod actor_count`. Observation rules, overlay, process.py, claim-then-eat
  621 latency and every world lever remain unchanged. No retained population state,
  622 memory, social action or new trait. Viewer HTML and text read saved native
  623 scores, decisions, rotation, requests and settlement effects without rescoring.
  624 
  625 **Fixed comparison and stop.** Three whole-world executions maximum: seed 7,
  626 300 ticks, yield ON, scoring ON, identical ON repetition, then scoring OFF.
  627 Declare commands/configuration/remaining budget before execution; fresh names,
  628 all attempts retained, no tuning, extra seeds, horizon extension or capacity
  629 runs. Compare OFF decisions and physical trajectory to saved leg 5, repeated ON
  630 canonical content excluding timing, and ON/OFF survivors, identified/timed deaths,
  631 denied claims, tick-start emergency person-ticks and yields. Report native
  632 GO/YIELD scores and consequences and explicitly whether GO actually wins while
  633 YIELD is eligible. Timings stand separately; changed digests are not behavioural
  634 evidence. Missing demonstrations must be reported without another run or a
  635 completion claim. Keep old reference assertions; focused fixtures are distinct
  636 from whole-world exploration executions.
  637 
  638 **Revised commit/push authority.** Commit on `codex/kernel-first-slice` and push
  639 only to the existing verified private origin. Verify both destination and private
  640 visibility before pushing. No remote creation, visibility change, merge or public
  641 release. If privacy cannot be verified, keep the local commit and report the
  642 blocker. This supersedes earlier no-push directions for this bounded work only.
  643 
  644 **Immediate next decision after C.** Present the recorded consequences of
  645 claim-then-eat latency and a bounded owner decision before ratification. Do not
  646 silently defer it into another behaviour leg or alter it during leg 6. C remains
  647 exploration output, not owner acceptance. No leg 7, orchestrator, social/memory
  648 work, ratification or Stage exit is opened.

  650 ### OD-012 - 2026-09-22 - Presentation track: world view, isometric smooth playback
  651 
  652 **Exact owner direction.** "Checkpoint C is much more technical than I wanted
  653 to look at. I want a 2.5D view of the world, much more polished than the
  654 previous sim's viewer, and it has to run smoothly, not jump tick to tick."
  655 Recorded from the owner's messages of 2026-09-22 accepting the drafted entry
  656 and brief ("you implement").
  657 
  658 **Amendment to OD-009.** "Readable living world" in OD-009 and Revision 3
  659 means two things from this entry on: instrument views (records, scores,
  660 rotation, settlement, as delivered at checkpoints A to C) and a world view a
  661 person can watch tick by tick. Checkpoints A to C stand as delivered on their
  662 declared instrument content. This entry neither accepts nor rejects
  663 checkpoint C; that remains a separate owner ruling, as does the claim-then-eat
  664 latency decision named in OD-011. Acceptance of any future checkpoint may
  665 cite the world view for readability; it may never cite the world view for a
  666 behavioural or accounting claim.
  667 
  668 **Scope.** Opens a presentation track alongside the exploration lane. It is
  669 not a leg, has no behavioural objective, and does not touch the exploration
  670 or ratification lanes' sequencing; leg 7 is not opened by it. It lives in a
  671 new top-level `viewer/` package. It proceeds on `codex/kernel-first-slice` in
  672 file-disjoint commits; no branch is created for it. For this track only, it
  673 amends ROADMAP.md's Stage 1 "no graphical viewer" and Stage 2 "observation is
  674 a text display of sealed native records, not a product" lines: the world view
  675 is a way to look, and the instrument views remain the record of observation.
  676 
  677 **Rules.**
  678 1. Reads saved run files only. Never live state, never a running engine.
  679 2. Imports nothing from `kernel/`, `world/` or `stream/` (`stream/` imports
  680    the kernel). The dependency-direction check is extended to assert this.
  681 3. One self-contained HTML file per rendered run, generated by a small Python
  682    script that inlines the run data, following the existing `world/viewer.py`
  683    pattern. No framework, no build step, no package install, no external
  684    resources.
  685 4. Interpolation is presentation. Positions and colours may be tweened
  686    between recorded ticks for smooth playback; nothing derived from an
  687    interpolated value is written, exported, counted, or shown in any
  688    instrument view. Events (claim, eat, yield, denial, death) snap to their
  689    recorded tick boundary. There are no half-tick states. When paused,
  690    stepped, or landed by the scrubber, the frame shows exactly the recorded
  691    tick state with no interpolation; the inspect panel shows recorded values
  692    and recorded band labels only, never a colour or a fraction.
  693 5. Not an acceptance instrument. Behavioural and accounting claims cite
  694    records and instrument views only. The world view does not verify a run
  695    file's digests; that is the reader's job (`stream.run_file.read_run`).
  696 
  697 **Definitions.** Tick index `t` in the page is the recorded state after `t`
  698 ticks (`t = 0` is genesis, `t = n` is the final state). During playback from
  699 `t` toward `t + 1` the counter shows `t`, positions and hunger colour tween
  700 from state `t` to state `t + 1`, and the decision, claims and settlement shown
  701 are those of tick `t` (the ones that produce state `t + 1`). A person whose
  702 recorded `died_at` is `k` moves during the interval ending at `k`, stops at
  703 the `k` boundary, and their death marker begins there and fades over the next
  704 three ticks.
  705 
  706 **First deliverable (v1).** An isometric (2:1 diamond tile) canvas render of
  707 the grid: cells, the source with its stock, shelters, and people as simple
  708 sprites; hunger band as colour (fed, hungry, emergency) using the run's
  709 declared thresholds; trait shown on the sprite; a hollow ring while yielding;
  710 a death marker that fades over three ticks; crowd count on the source cell.
  711 Playback: play, pause, speed from 1 to 20 ticks per second, scrubber that
  712 lands on integer ticks, single step forward and back, tick counter.
  713 Smoothness: requestAnimationFrame, each person tweened between the recorded
  714 cell at tick `t` and tick `t + 1` across the playback interval with an ease,
  715 camera pan and zoom, fit-to-grid. Inspect: click a person for their recorded
  716 decision line, hunger, stock and trait at the current tick; click the source
  717 for stock and this tick's accepted and denied claims. A legend and a footer
  718 line stating that motion between ticks is interpolated presentation. Must
  719 play the 300-tick seed-7 files without visible jank and must be built so that
  720 50 people over 2,000 ticks would still play at full frame rate.
  721 
  722 **Deferred polish.** Sprite animation frames, terrain art, lighting, weather,
  723 sound, paths and trails, night and day. These are reopened by owner direction
  724 once a Stage 2 world is viable, so polish is spent on a world people live in.
  725 
  726 **Budget.** No new whole-world executions; render the existing leg-6 seed-7
  727 ON and OFF files (`runs/leg6-20260922-seed7-300-on.jsonl`,
  728 `runs/leg6-20260922-seed7-300-off.jsonl`). One sitting. Existing stage
  729 budgets unchanged. A synthetic 50-person, 2,000-tick fixture written by the
  730 test suite in the run-file shape is a test input, not a world execution, and
  731 is never saved under `runs/` or cited as a run.
  732 
  733 **Tests.** Embedded run data digest equals the source file digest; the
  734 generated HTML references no external resource; dependency-direction check
  735 passes with `viewer/` included; JavaScript syntax check; the generator
  736 refuses a run file whose header it does not recognise rather than guessing;
  737 the synthetic 50 x 2,000 fixture renders and the page stays under a declared
  738 size bound; the per-frame interpolation step is timed headlessly on that
  739 fixture. Layout and motion are inspected by the owner; the report lists that
  740 as unverified by the agent if the browser tool cannot open local files.
  741 
  742 **Stop.** v1 renders both seed-7 files, plays smoothly at 10 ticks per second
  743 on the owner's machine, and the report is delivered. Anything beyond v1
  744 waits for a further direction.
  745 
  746 **Standing.** Does not block leg 7. Does not amend DOCTRINE.md. Commit and
  747 push under the revised push authority (OD-011) once the origin's visibility
  748 is settled; until then, local commit and report.
  749 
  750 **Reason.** The exploration lane's purpose is a world the owner can see.
  751 Checkpoints A to C delivered records the reviewer can read; the owner needs
  752 the picture as well, and the 02 line showed that watching the world is what
  753 drives good direction. The rules keep the viewer from becoming what the
  754 previous frontend became.
  755 
  756 **Linked revision.** Recorded on `codex/kernel-first-slice` after
  757 `94523ae06957a549ffd2b98aacb3b12120f6b0f3`. The recording commit's hash
  758 cannot appear inside this entry and is reported after the commit.

  792 
  793 **Exact owner direction.** Recorded from the owner's message of 2026-09-23:
  794 
  795 > Checkpoint C is accepted as sufficient to close the current exploration
  796 > question.
  797 >
  798 > For claim-then-eat latency:
  799 >
  800 > KEEP CLAIM/OBTAIN AND EAT AS SEPARATE ACTIONS.
  801 >
  802 > Do not implement same-tick obtain-and-eat.
  803 >
  804 > Rationale:
  805 > - obtaining/possessing food and consuming food are distinct causal events;
  806 > - the intermediate possession state is valuable world state;
  807 > - preserving it allows later systems to interact with possession before
  808 >   consumption, including transfer, storage, theft, interruption,
  809 >   prioritisation, social behaviour, and resource competition;
  810 > - visual convenience or apparent one-tick awkwardness is not sufficient
  811 >   justification to collapse two causal actions;
  812 > - no behavioural/kernel change is authorised by this decision.
  813 >
  814 > This is an owner design decision, not experimental proof.
  815

  819 authority returns to ROADMAP.md. Exploration leg 7 is not opened and would
  820 need a new owner direction. Obtain/claim and eat remain separate actions, as
  821 OD-013 already recorded; this entry supplies the owner's rationale and does
  822 not amend OD-013, the leg-6 evidence, or the availability boundary. No
  823 same-tick obtain-and-eat revision is authorised. No kernel, world, stream,
  824 viewer, or tooling change is authorised. The Stage 1 exit is not accepted.
  825 Capability, reachability, and measurement are not established by this entry.

  839 on 2026-09-23. The builder's browser inspection of checkpoint C had been
  840 blocked by URL policy; it is superseded by the owner's inspection, not by
  841 any re-run, and the leg-6 run budget remains exhausted. Observation the
  842 owner carries forward as an input, not a result: in the ON run the source
  843 sits at its cap (8/8) at tick 300 while the living people are hungry and

===== ROADMAP.md =====
    1 # V3 — development roadmap
    2 
    3 **ADOPTED 2026-09-19; scope updated 2026-09-21 under OD-008 and OD-009
    4 Revision 3, and on 2026-09-23 under OD-014. No stage has passed. OD-014
    5 accepted visual checkpoint C and closed OD-009 Revision 3, so sequencing
    6 authority is this document. Stage 1 continues at slice 1c, then slice 1d,
    7 then the Stage 1 exit review. That closure does not open a later stage, an
    8 exploration leg, or execution by itself. Confirmation floors, frozen
    9 baselines and independent acceptance belong to the ratification lane. This
   10 document by itself authorises no execution.**
   11 
   12 The behavioural starter world has six people. The engineering target is 50 people.
   13 The order below follows dependencies, not the old project's feature sequence.
   14 Capacity workloads cannot supply behavioural counts for Stages 3–5.
   15 
   16 ## The active stage record
   17 
   18 Keep a one-page working card at the top of the stage record, followed by definitions,
   19 an attempt ledger, results, and review. Use the template at the end of this document.
   20 The card is an index into evidence, not another governing document. Fill definitions
   21 only for the active stage; later stages do not need invented values now.
   22 
   23 No exploratory, confirmation, or benchmark run begins with an unspecified budget,
   24 threshold, counting rule, or horizon. Positive exit claims and integrity rails must
   25 pass; optional targets cannot excuse their absence.
   26 
   27 ### Adopted operating budgets
   28 
   29 These are adopted ceilings, not measured capabilities or confidence estimates.
   30 They bound exploration cost. Stage 2 has the largest search allowance because it
   31 must establish viability and opportunity together.
   32 
   33 | Work | Candidate limit | Whole-world execution limit | Maximum ticks per execution |
   34 |---|---:|---:|---:|
   35 | Stage 2 world design | 8 world configurations | 24 | 10,000 |
   36 | Stage 3 request policy | 3 policy configurations | 12 | 10,000 |
   37 | Stage 4 memory policy | 3 policy configurations | 18 | 10,000 |
   38 | Stage 5 observation | 1 fixed model | 6 | 30,000 |
   39 | Capacity checks, each of Stages 1–4 | 1 workload specification per stage | 8 | 10,000 |
   40 
   41 Each execution, including confirmation, invalid runs, review reruns, and each arm
   42 of a paired comparison, consumes a slot. Stage 4's extra slots accommodate the
   43 intervention and no-op controls. A world/configuration combination counts
   44 once as a candidate even if tested on several declared inputs. Reserve confirmation
   45 and review capacity before exploration. Horizon extension consumes another execution;
   46 never continue a confirmation until it happens to pass. Focused transaction tests
   47 and analysis of existing sealed evidence do not consume whole-world slots.
   48 
   49 Keep a shared ledger for questions spanning stages. Stage 3 does not inherit a fresh
   50 world-design allowance: failure of its opportunities revalidates the Stage 2 world
   51 within the remaining world budget. A bounded exploratory counterfactual continuation
   52 counts as an execution arm; repeated focal-choice evaluations without ticking are
   53 instrument tests. Version changes, retries, and session changes never renew budgets.
   54 
   55 Adopted capacity ceilings on one named reference machine: 50 actors; mean tick
   56 time at most 50 ms and 95th percentile at most 100 ms; peak process memory at most
   57 512 MiB; complete uncompressed replay plus decision evidence averaging at most
   58 64 KiB per tick and at most 1 GiB for 10,000 ticks. At fixed population and bounded
   59 state, peak memory may grow by at most 64 MiB between 1,000 and 10,000 ticks.
   60 These are operational targets, not predictions: the storage limit keeps a benchmark
   61 under 1 GiB, and the memory limit rules out keeping its entire history in RAM.
   62 Record machine, runtime, workload,
   63 measurement method, and exact horizons before benchmarking. Include settlement
   64 and evidence writes in cost; report final flush and both evidence streams separately.
   65 Missing a ceiling cannot be fixed by dropping required evidence.
   66 
   67 ## 1. Kernel only
   68 
   69 Build immutable state views, proposals, deterministic resolution, validated atomic
   70 effects, action settlement, immutable evidence, and replay. A minimal actor has an
   71 immutable ID, stock ownership or declared permission to claim a shared source,
   72 and a proposal interface; it is not yet a person domain. No needs, movement,
   73 perception behaviour, social systems, construction, graphical viewer, plugin
   74 platform, or speculative general scheduler.
   75 
   76 Declare units, numeric rules, canonical serialization, and code/schema versions
   77 before implementation. Identity and replay must not depend on incidental object
   78 order, wall-clock timing, or platform-specific number formatting.
   79 
   80 ### Four internal slices, one stage exit
   81 
   82 | Slice | Proof object |
   83 |---|---|
   84 | 1a | Two authorised claimants, one unit, one winner; invalid writes and observer mutation cannot commit. |
   85 | 1b | Two-tick reserve → complete or cancel → release; exactly-once settlement. |
   86 | 1c | Sealed evidence, replay, recovery, and private instance isolation. |
   87 | 1d | The declared 50-actor cost envelope against the frozen 1a–1c reference suite. |
   88 
   89 Complete them in order. They are implementation slices, not extra roadmap stages
   90 or approval gates. The first code is the contested transaction and isolation checks.
   91 
   92 ### Tick algorithm
   93 
   94 1. Freeze the tick-start state and declared boundary inputs. Build views and collect
   95    proposals against this boundary. Later people receive only their bounded view.
   96 2. Validate proposal shape, unique identity, authority, and operation preconditions.
   97    Reject all proposals sharing a duplicate ID, regardless of which arrived first.
   98 3. Apply authorised cancellation causes before completion of the same action.
   99    Cancellation releases its reservation exactly once. Rejecting an unrelated
  100    proposal does not cancel ongoing work.
  101 4. Resolve surviving reserved completions, then new transactions. Within each phase,
  102    sort the tick-start actor roster by stable ID and rotate it left by
  103    `tick mod actor_count` (empty roster: no actor transactions). The total key is
  104    phase, rotated actor rank, then engine-assigned actor-local proposal sequence.
  105    Use one global order for multi-resource transactions. Proposal sequences must
  106    not depend on collection order or incidental input-map iteration.
  107 5. Validate each entire transaction against remaining tick-start spendable balances
  108    or its own reservation. Acceptance reduces that availability immediately;
  109    rejection leaves it unchanged. Stage balanced effects and lifecycle changes,
  110    committing each accepted transaction completely or not at all.
  111 6. Commit the next state and seal the tick's evidence. Publish immutable records.
  112 
  113 New credits, production, and released prior reservations become spendable at the
  114 next tick-start. Existing reservations may settle through their owning action.
  115 An atomic transaction may have several balanced effects, but cannot spend an
  116 incoming credit as if already held. Reservation is neither consumption nor extra
  117 stock; count held or escrowed units once. Validate source balances and conservation
  118 before commit; never repair an invalid transition with clamping.
  119 
  120 Stage 2 declares how mandatory need changes and death generate boundary outcomes
  121 and cancellation causes; rejected voluntary actions cannot suppress those changes.
  122 Equivalent partner ties use tick-based rotation of the sorted eligible partner
  123 list. Rotation is deterministic priority, not a fairness guarantee.
  124 
  125 **Required credit fixture:** A owns one unit, B owns none. At T, A→B is A's first
  126 proposal and is accepted. A's subsequent claim on that unit is denied. B's consume
  127 proposal at T is denied regardless of resolver order. At T+1, B may consume if the
  128 unit remains held. Production and reservation release obey the same availability
  129 boundary. Partial availability rejects an indivisible transaction; it cannot create
  130 an implicit partial credit. Also test repeated completion and cancellation versus
  131 completion at the same boundary. Cancellation, completion, denial, and transfer
  132 must carry explicit outcome reasons rather than disappear from the record.
  133 
  134 ### Evidence and foundation checks
  135 
  136 Specify the small evidence schema before implementing its writer: genesis and
  137 code/configuration/schema identities; ordered inputs and tick IDs; proposal/action
  138 IDs; resolution reasons; balanced effects and reservation transitions; state/event
  139 identities; and a complete-tick seal. Native decision records contain or reference
  140 immutable copies of the actual inputs and alternatives used, linked to outcomes.
  141 They are not later reconstructions from live state.
  142 
  143 Stream required evidence. Optional diagnostic capture cannot change canonical
  144 simulation history, state, or identity allocation. A partial write recovers only
  145 through the last verified sealed tick; resume with pending reservations must agree
  146 with uninterrupted execution. Replay independently from genesis and declared inputs;
  147 snapshots accelerate recovery without replacing that check.
  148 
  149 Verify input-map permutation, repeated execution, replay and resume; unauthorised
  150 writes; observer attempts to mutate live or prior state; and isolation of two
  151 instances restored from the same state. Changing one instance cannot affect the
  152 other. This enables Stage 4's small intervention harness without building a generic
  153 branching platform now. Add dependency-direction checks and the reference suite.
  154 Old accounting/observer failures are adversarial examples, not a queue of old domains.
  155 
  156 After 1a–1c, benchmark 50 synthetic actors with fixed contention/action mixes at
  157 1,000 and 10,000 ticks, twice at each length. Use the declared cost ceilings, stream
  158 complete evidence, and retain no ever-growing history in working memory.
  159 
  160 ## 2. Viable world with reachable social opportunities
  161 
  162 Build a compact six-person world with movement, bounded perception, food, water,
  163 warmth, shelter, and renewable resources. Shelters may be placed; construction is
  164 not required. Observation is a text display of sealed native records, not a product.
  165 
  166 Declare the world-design budget first. Allowed levers are geometry, distribution,
  167 renewal, initial supplies, perception radius, and consumption rates. Do not add a
  168 social system to create need. Separate an accounting defect from a world-design miss.
  169 
  170 Declare the initial placement and stock-generation rules before exploration; they
  171 must not assign successful pairs or advantages by actor identity. Designed maps and
  172 hand-built diagnostic fixtures are allowed, but fixtures cannot supply ordinary-world
  173 confirmation counts. Freeze geometry, ranges, initialisation rules, and confirmation
  174 inputs before confirmation. Do not nudge individual coordinates after seeing a miss.
  175 Changing an allowed world lever creates a recorded candidate within the same budget.
  176 
  177 Exit evidence must show repeated ordinary obtain/consume cycles after starting
  178 reserves cease to explain survival, exact accounting, and the declared survival
  179 rail. A deprived fixture must still suffer or die under the declared need rules.
  180 Define explicit safety rails and measure emergencies as well as deaths; merely
  181 keeping everyone barely alive does not establish the intended viable baseline.
  182 Need progression and world processes continue when a voluntary action is rejected;
  183 losing contention cannot freeze a person's hunger or grant a free pause.
  184 
  185 The ordinary world must produce prospective help opportunities in at least six
  186 distinct shortfall episodes across three people, using Stage 3's episode definition.
  187 Count only frames where the person can make a decision, meets the proposed request
  188 eligibility, and observes at least two potential partners with spare food inside
  189 the declared interaction range. Define spare-stock and knowledge-freshness rules
  190 before counting. Mid-action frames and unusable alternatives do not qualify.
  191 These are prospective opportunities, not completed requests. Use engine accessors
  192 and the actual tick-start observations; do not infer opportunities from later stock
  193 or locations. The counting instrument must pass known-positive, known-negative,
  194 busy-actor, stale-knowledge, and boundary cases before its totals are used.
  195 
  196 Before advancing, write explicit score inequalities for a region where the proposed
  197 request can beat ordinary competing actions without overriding an emergency. Back
  198 the argument with constructed inputs evaluated through the shared selection rule.
  199 Include exclusions and tie-breaking, not just raw scores. Do not build a second
  200 scorer in the probe. The probe may implement the proposed request candidate's
  201 eligibility and score for read-only evaluation; Stage 3 reuses that exact logic.
  202 It enables no social execution in Stage 2. This establishes possibility; Stage 3
  203 must demonstrate actual selection and completion after social actions alter the world.
  204 
  205 ### Definitions to freeze before the first counting run
  206 
  207 Keep these in the stage record with exact values in its configuration. Freeze
  208 counting predicates before exploration; declare which physical parameters may vary.
  209 
  210 | Term | Required definition |
  211 |---|---|
  212 | Spare food | Unreserved stock exceeding the giver's declared retained amount, in named units; observed estimates and execution-time availability remain distinct. |
  213 | Range and freshness | Distance metric, perception radius, interaction/contact range, maximum observation age, and boundary inclusivity. |
  214 | Shortfall episode | Exact start and recovery predicates, hysteresis if used, and handling of death, run-end censoring, and initially hungry actors. A dead actor does not recover. |
  215 | Emergency | Exact condition giving mandatory survival precedence; ordinary competitors are the other genuinely eligible engine candidates. |
  216 | Viability | Observation horizon, renewal/need-cycle duration, minimum survival and emergency rails, and the check that starting buffers are insufficient to explain the result. |
  217 | Opportunity | Decision-ready actor, request eligibility, and two observed usable partners; count actor/tick and episode separately. |
  218 
  219 Later, Stage 4 adds the exact separation duration, contact predicate, memory access
  220 scope, material outcome, and persistence window before its exploration. These are
  221 stage-specific modelling declarations, not missing prerequisites for kernel work.
  222 
  223 Repeat the 50-person engineering workload with movement and perception active.
  224 A synthetic kernel benchmark alone does not establish full-world capacity.
  225 
  226 ## 3. First social behaviour: request food
  227 
  228 Implement observation, eligible partners, selection, help/refusal, and settlement.
  229 Memory influence is disabled. Counterpart consent and available stock are checked
  230 at the declared execution boundary; an observed spare is not a guaranteed delivery.
  231 Test refusal, competition, movement away, interruption, and death boundaries as
  232 applicable. Outstanding obligations cannot duplicate or disappear silently.
  233 
  234 ### Adopted confirmation floors
  235 
  236 | Measure | Minimum |
  237 |---|---:|
  238 | Actual scored food-request frames | 12 |
  239 | Distinct shortfall episodes | 6 across at least 3 people |
  240 | Request selections with at least 2 eligible partners | 6, from distinct episodes across at least 3 people |
  241 | Completed transfers | 3 across at least 2 recipients |
  242 
  243 Count at most one request frame per actor per tick. An episode begins when the
  244 declared hunger-shortfall predicate becomes true and ends when the triggering need
  245 reaches its declared recovery condition. Repeated asks, waiting, changing partners,
  246 or receipt of food before recovery do not create another episode. Record the exact
  247 start/recovery predicates before exploration; do not change them to improve counts.
  248 
  249 Every counted selection must be the actual engine winner. Report the five-link
  250 funnel, refusals and interruptions, with actors, episodes, and denominators explicit.
  251 Include per-person counts and episode duration, candidate count, request delay,
  252 and transferred-quantity distributions; retain zeroes and censored episodes.
  253 Do not pool successful exploration into confirmation. Transfers conserve stock;
  254 survival and integrity rails pass. Check the capacity workload with requests active.
  255 
  256 These are engineering coverage floors, not statistical confidence thresholds.
  257 Freeze the horizon and counting rules before confirmation. More selected asks alone
  258 do not demonstrate memory, relationships, or emergent cooperation.
  259 
  260 ## 4. Experience changes material outcomes
  261 
  262 Use earned interaction memory to change partner choice on the demonstrated world.
  263 The first model stores dated food-request outcomes with counterpart, observation
  264 time, and source event ID: at most 32 records per person and four per counterpart.
  265 Evict oldest first, breaking same-tick ties by stable event ID; enforce both caps.
  266 Declare expiry and the bounded scoring rule before exploration. Query work is
  267 bounded by those 32 records. No embeddings, inferred friendship store, or general
  268 belief engine. These caps are design proposals, not evidence of adequate memory.
  269 Social-memory eviction cannot erase transaction obligations held by the kernel.
  270 
  271 Require three complete causal chains across at least two people: ordinary accepted
  272 interaction creates memory; declared separation follows; removing access only to
  273 the specified memory at the same later decision changes the choice; ordinary
  274 execution accepts the changed action; and a declared material consequence persists
  275 in canonical state over the specified window.
  276 Count distinct later need episodes, not repeated attempts inside one episode.
  277 Record the originating interaction and retained information supporting each chain.
  278 
  279 Replay to just before the focal choice and restore private instances from identical
  280 state. Declare whether the intervention masks one source event or one counterpart's
  281 buffer, and how focal choices are selected before confirmation. Mask only that
  282 actor's access at that decision; do not delete physical state, transaction records,
  283 or other people's memories. Restore ordinary access for later decisions.
  284 Prove both an unmodified fork and an empty-mask intervention reproduce the original
  285 choice and continuation. Keep physical state and all other decision inputs fixed
  286 at the focal choice; divergence after a changed action is then part of the outcome.
  287 Do not attribute a whole-run difference to memory without tracing that choice's
  288 downstream effects. Pure memory fields, identifiers, and diagnostic counters are
  289 not material consequences.
  290 
  291 Declare separation, relevant expiry conditions, consequence dimensions, and the
  292 persistence window before confirmation, justified by Stage 2's action/need timescales.
  293 Separation uses the declared contact range and minimum duration, not merely different
  294 coordinates or an arbitrary age counter. State whether post-expiry influence is
  295 required; an in-window effect cannot satisfy a post-expiry claim.
  296 Use confirmation inputs not selected for success during exploration. A seed-label
  297 change counts as variation only when it changes a real input or random sequence.
  298 
  299 Check capacity/eviction boundaries and repeat the 50-person workload with memory
  300 active. Report demonstrated mechanism, observed occurrence, and robustness separately.
  301 
  302 ## 5. Observe longer-term patterns
  303 
  304 Hold local rules fixed over declared need and encounter cycles. Measure recurring
  305 partner preference, avoidance, reciprocity, association, or no durable pattern.
  306 Repeated measurements of one encounter are not independent relationship episodes.
  307 
  308 Introduce no friendship object merely to produce the desired finding. One leftover
  309 unit of food is not evidence of a durable relationship.
  310 Distinguish association explained by geography from experience-dependent association.
  311 Attribute a recurring pattern to memory only when an appropriate control supports it.
  312 
  313 A valid negative observation may complete this observation stage. It does not
  314 establish the desired pattern or mean the project's full purpose has been achieved.
  315 The population/horizon of behavioural evidence limits its claim; a 50-person
  316 performance benchmark is not a 50-person social demonstration.
  317 
  318 ## 6. Choose one extension
  319 
  320 The owner chooses the next objective from the recorded limitations and desired
  321 experience. Name the consumer, ordinary opportunity, consequence, and check before
  322 implementation. No automatic feature train resumes.
  323 
  324 ## Misses, stop conditions, and progression
  325 
  326 - No opportunity: examine the world and eligibility.
  327 - Opportunity exists, action loses: examine the competing reasons.
  328 - Action wins, execution fails: examine execution and accounting.
  329 - Action completes, consequence disappears: examine the claimed causal path.
  330 - Measurement is unreliable: make one bounded instrument-repair attempt within the
  331   remaining budget. If validation still fails, the affected claim is UNKNOWN.
  332 
  333 An unresolved prerequisite blocks dependent work. Stages 3 and 4 require positive
  334 demonstrations; a well-measured absence cannot pass them. Stage 5 permits a negative
  335 observation because observation is its declared objective.
  336 
  337 Two confirmation misses at the same causal link halt the stage for a bounded owner
  338 revision even if execution slots remain. Exploratory misses consume the search
  339 budget without becoming extra approval gates. No downstream work bypasses a failed
  340 or unknown prerequisite.
  341 
  342 At budget exhaustion, report the missing link and bounded alternatives: revise
  343 world assumptions, revise the local policy, choose another first behaviour, or
  344 suspend the objective. No silent budget extension or new prerequisite programme.
  345 
  346 Progression also requires the independent evidence review specified in AGENTS.md.
  347 Keep one stage record with the design, versions, original criteria, raw evidence,
  348 instrument checks, failed attempts, review, outcome, and rollback.
  349 
  350 ## Stage-card template
  351 
  352 Use these eight rows; link details rather than expanding the card into another plan.
  353 Keep one configuration file per attempt and an artifact index beneath the card.
  354 
  355 | Field | Contents |
  356 |---|---|
  357 | State | Stage/slice, active objective and consumer, prerequisite/review status. |
  358 | Scope | In/out, owning modules, permitted changes. |
  359 | Identity | Source, configuration and schema versions; owner-direction reference. |
  360 | Claim | Essential exit conditions and integrity rails; optional targets separately. |
  361 | Definitions | Linked predicates, units, tie rules, counting boundaries and input selection. |
  362 | Instrument | Known cases, null controls, accessors, native inputs and validation result. |
  363 | Budget and attempt | Limits/used/remaining; exact runs, horizons, reserved confirmation/review slots. |
  364 | Evidence and rollback | Commands, artifacts, result/review links; exact files/configuration to revert. |
  365 
  366 ### Filled example: illustrative card, not execution evidence
  367 
  368 The table below is a worked illustration of the template written before any
  369 repository existed. It is not the Stage 1a record. The live Stage 1a record is
  370 `evidence/stage-01/RECORD.md`.
  371 
  372 | Field | Stage 1a example |
  373 |---|---|
  374 | State | Draft; contested shared-stock transaction consumed by two synthetic actors. Nothing implemented or passed. |
  375 | Scope | Minimal state, proposal validation, deterministic settlement, immutable observation and focused tests. No person domain, reservations, replay platform, or world runs. |
  376 | Identity | These three draft documents; no repository/code revision exists yet. Builder records the actual initial revision before tests. |
  377 | Claim | One source unit yields exactly one credited unit; balances remain nonnegative; invalid/unauthorised transactions and observer mutation cannot alter canonical state. |
  378 | Definitions | Integer resource units; tick starts at zero; authorised shared-source claims; roadmap rotation; all-or-nothing transactions. |
  379 | Instrument | Inspect actual pre/post state and accepted/denied outcomes; reverse map/proposal collection order; attempt observer mutation; compare diagnostics on/off. Not run. |
  380 | Budget and attempt | Focused deterministic fixtures only; zero whole-world or capacity runs authorised for this slice. |
  381 | Evidence and rollback | Planned record: evidence/stage-01/RECORD.md. Builder records exact changed files, commands/results and rollback; Stage 1 exit review remains pending. |

===== DOCTRINE.md =====
    1 # V3 — founding doctrine
    2 
    3 **ADOPTED 2026-09-19 with AGENTS.md and ROADMAP.md. Effective and mandatory.**
    4 
    5 ## Purpose
    6 
    7 Build complete, understandable behaviours whose interactions may produce larger
    8 patterns. A new implementation is the owner's chosen starting point. Its value
    9 must be demonstrated through correct accounting, clear ownership, native decision
   10 evidence, and bounded operating costs; a blank repository proves none of these.
   11 
   12 ## Ten principles
   13 
   14 1. **Complete the causal behaviour.** Follow an ordinary event through decision,
   15    execution or explicit refusal, and its consequences. Waiting, refusal, and death
   16    are valid outcomes; needs do not pause because a voluntary action failed.
   17    A positive capability claim still needs its stated successful causal chain.
   18    A stored field, candidate score, animation, or changed hash is insufficient.
   19 
   20 2. **Establish opportunity before influence.** Demonstrate a consuming decision
   21    with meaningful alternatives before testing memory's effect. Track observation,
   22    eligibility, ranking, execution, and consequence separately. There is one live
   23    selection implementation. Probes may call it or inspect its native records;
   24    a parallel scorer is not evidence of what the engine chose.
   25 
   26 3. **World design is legitimate work.** Declared exploration may vary geography,
   27    resource conditions, perception, consumption, and local rules within the active
   28    objective. Ordinary decisions must determine participants and outcomes. Do not
   29    script successful pairs, encounters, transfers, or reunions.
   30 
   31 4. **Exploration is bounded; confirmation is frozen.** Declare the question,
   32    allowed changes, and budget before exploration. Declare activation, eligibility,
   33    and ranking changes before implementing them. Freeze code, configuration,
   34    criteria, inputs, horizon, and analysis before confirmation. Further tuning
   35    preserves the old result and consumes the existing budget; it cannot rescue
   36    an earlier result retrospectively.
   37 
   38 5. **Correctness cannot be switched off.** Every resource gain has a source;
   39    production, consumption, and loss have explicit rules. Allocation, reservations,
   40    interruption, rejection, and completion conserve the declared quantities.
   41    Invalid transitions cannot commit. Domains cannot bypass settlement by writing
   42    balances directly. Clamping cannot conceal invalid credits.
   43 
   44 6. **Knowledge is local and dated.** Decisions use the person's bounded view of
   45    the relevant tick-start state, not unrestricted world access. Retained facts
   46    carry observation time and a simple source identifier. Acting on stale knowledge
   47    may fail visibly. Do not add a general belief-revision system without a need.
   48 
   49 7. **Evidence is native and inert.** Capture relevant inputs, eligible alternatives,
   50    selection, and outcome at their actual engine boundaries. Inspection cannot
   51    mutate simulation state. Validate instruments against real engine decisions,
   52    nulls, known differences, and deliberate failure cases before interpreting runs.
   53    Use meaningful denominators when compared trajectories diverge.
   54 
   55 8. **Local rules are authored; patterns are findings.** A programmed preference is
   56    a model assumption. Cooperation, attachment, avoidance, and spatial association
   57    are claims to test. Absence of a pattern is a valid observation.
   58 
   59 9. **Enforce ownership through tests and review.** Separate state, perception,
   60    candidate generation, selection, resolution, and evidence responsibilities.
   61    Test dependency direction.
   62    Review semantic ownership as well: correct imports do not excuse behavioural
   63    calculations accumulating inside person orchestration. Build only abstractions
   64    needed by the active slice.
   65 
   66 10. **Evidence remains honest.** Preserve failed and superseded results. Never
   67     weaken assertions or replace a reference result to manufacture a pass. Label
   68     invalid measurements UNKNOWN for the affected claim. Distinguish a demonstrated
   69     mechanism, its ordinary frequency, and its robustness across conditions.
   70 
   71 ## Behavioural freedom and reference results
   72 
   73 Capability rules may be revised through declared experiments. Mandatory invariants
   74 may not. A reference suite checks byte equality for changes claimed to be inert.
   75 For intentional behavioural changes, declare and explain expected differences,
   76 retain prior results, and review new expectations. Do not preserve a correctness
   77 defect behind an off switch merely to keep old outputs identical.
   78 
   79 Snapshots may accelerate recovery. Replay from the initial definition and declared
   80 inputs remains independently verifiable. State belongs to an engine instance;
   81 isolated copies cannot share mutable state or observational references.
   82 
   83 Use no runtime randomness through Stage 3. A genesis generator may use a named,
   84 versioned deterministic algorithm and seed, with the resulting initial state saved.
   85 Later randomness requires a declared modelling need, replayable state and consumption,
   86 and an owner direction; it cannot be introduced to rescue coverage counts.
   87 
   88 Break equivalent competing claims and partner ties by a declared, replayable rule
   89 that rotates priority across ticks rather than permanently favouring a fixed ID.
   90 Identity may stabilise ordering; it must not confer permanent priority. Rotation
   91 does not prove fairness when opportunity or eligibility differs.
   92 
   93 The new engine has no obligation to reproduce the old project's trajectories.

   28 
   29 ### Slice 1c declaration — 2026-09-23, before code
   30 
   31 Authority: OD-013 (ratification lane, slice 1c) and OD-015 (boundary: the
   32 ROADMAP 1c proof object). Specification: ROADMAP.md "Evidence and foundation
   33 checks". Written before any 1c writer or reader change. A value here changes
   34 only by a new dated declaration appended below, never by editing this one.
   35 
   36 Read in the code at `6f50040` before writing. Tick lines already hold the
   37 full committed kernel state, the overlay and any production entries.
   38 `WorldState` carries its tick, so rotation and action identities come back
   39 with the state. `Engine` holds only its current state, a diagnostics counter
   40 and a lock, so an engine built on a restored state continues identically
   41 without an engine change. The scenario generator's PRNG state is not in the
   42 file. The scenario submits a boolean amount on purpose, which the kernel
   43 canonical form refuses. `viewer/world_view.py` recognises only v3.stream.2 and
   44 rejects unknown line kinds. The first 120 tick lines of existing v3.stream.2
   45 files take about 0.28 MB (world, seed 7) and 0.59 MB (scenario, seed 7, whose
   46 holds accumulate).
   47 
   48 1. **Format and header.** `STREAM_FORMAT` becomes v3.stream.3 and
   49    `READABLE_FORMATS` gains it; v3.stream.1 and v3.stream.2 files stay
   50    readable with no seal checks. The header keeps every v3.stream.2 field and
   51    adds `horizon` (the declared tick count); `code_identity`
   52    (`{"files": {path: sha256}, "digest": digest(files)}` over every `*.py`
   53    under kernel/, stream/ and world/ at run time, paths relative and
   54    `/`-separated, bytes hashed after CRLF→LF); `config_identity` (the digest
   55    of the header's `scenario` description); and `seal`. No git head, dirty
   56    flag, clock or host enters the file: each would enter every seal and stop a
   57    reviewer reproducing a reference at another commit; the results entry names
   58    the commit of each reference. Engine and schema versions stay as they are,
   59    because nothing in settlement or the state schema changes.
   60 
   61 2. **Inputs.** Each tick line carries `inputs`: the proposals submitted to
   62    `Engine.tick` that tick, in submission order, each
   63    `{"proposal_id", "actor", "order", "operation", "params"}`, and `[]` when
   64    there are none. `params` is the canonical form when the submitted params
   65    have one. Otherwise the entry carries `params_typed` instead, where every
   66    value is a two-item list `[tag, payload]`: `int`, `str`, `none`, `bool`
   67    (1 or 0), `float` (`float.hex`), `seq` (encoded items) and `map`
   68    (`[encoded key, encoded value]` pairs sorted by the canonical bytes of the
   69    encoded key). Either form decodes to a `Proposal` equal to the one
   70    submitted, and the writer refuses anything else. Inputs link to outcomes by
   71    `proposal_id`. `decisions` and `observations` are unchanged.
   72 
   73 3. **Seal.** `header_seal = digest(header without "seal")`. Each tick line
   74    stores `seal = digest({"prev": prev, "tick": digest(tick payload without
   75    "seal")})`, where `prev` is `header_seal` for the first tick and the
   76    previous tick's seal after that. The end line adds `final_seal` (the last
   77    tick's seal, or `header_seal` when there are no ticks) beside `ticks` and
   78    `trail_digest`, and `ticks` must equal `horizon`. Timing lines stay outside
   79    every seal and digest. The trail digest is kept and computed as now, over
   80    the tick lines as written. Seals and inputs exist only in the stream: no
   81    kernel canonical form or digest changes.
   82 
   83 4. **Reader.** `read_run` keeps every existing check, verifies v3.stream.3
   84    seals, reports the first failing line, and exposes `last_sealed_tick`: the
   85    tick number of the last tick line whose seal, and every seal before it,
   86    verifies and which passed every other check on its line; -1 when the header
   87    seal fails or no tick qualifies; `None` for v3.stream.1/2 files. Nothing
   88    after a break counts as sealed.
   89 
   90 5. **Reconstruction.** Additive classmethods only: `Effect`, `Source`,
   91    `Reservation` and `WorldState.from_canonical` in kernel/state.py and
   92    `Overlay.from_canonical` in world/overlay.py. Each requires the exact
   93    canonical key set, and the current `schema_version` for `WorldState`. Each
   94    builds through the existing constructor, so the existing `__post_init__`
   95    rails validate it, and satisfies
   96    `from_canonical(x.canonical()).digest() == x.digest()`. No other kernel
   97    change. `Scenario.from_describe` and `WorldConfig.from_describe` require
   98    `describe()` of the result to equal the header description exactly, which
   99    also refuses unknown names, versions and generators.
  100 
  101 6. **Replay.** Rebuild the configuration from the header and re-execute from
  102    the header genesis. Scenario runs resubmit each tick's recorded inputs;
  103    world runs recompute observations, decisions and inputs with the live code.
  104    Compare every tick payload without `seal` byte for byte with the file, and
  105    report the first divergent tick and top-level field or `replay:
  106    identical`, together with whether the live code identity equals the
  107    header's. Only complete, fully sealed files are replayed. `--twice` stays
  108    and is not the ratification check. Commands:
  109    `py -3 -B -m stream.run --replay FILE` for scenario runs and
  110    `py -3 -B -m world.run --replay FILE` for world runs.
  111 
  112 7. **Recovery.** For a v3.stream.3 file that is cut or whose seal chain
  113    breaks, keep the file's bytes through the last sealed tick line (header,
  114    sealed ticks and the timing lines between them). Restore that tick's
  115    committed state, or its produced state when the line records production,
  116    with `WorldState.from_canonical`, and for world runs the overlay with
  117    `Overlay.from_canonical`. Continue to `horizon` in a new file. Recovery
  118    never reads past that line or uses an unsealed one. It refuses when the
  119    header seal fails or the live code identity differs from the header's. The
  120    scenario generator is not in the file, so recovery rebuilds it from the
  121    header's `Scenario` and runs it through the sealed ticks, requiring its
  122    output to equal each sealed tick's recorded inputs, before generating the
  123    rest. Every content line of the result (header, tick and end lines, seals
  124    included) must equal the uninterrupted run's; only timing lines may differ.
  125    Provenance (source sha256, last sealed tick, resumed range) is printed by
  126    the command and recorded in the results, not written into the file, so no
  127    new line kind is added. Commands: `--recover FILE --out NEW` on the same
  128    two runners.
  129 
  130 8. **Isolation.** Two engines restored from one sealed tick through
  131    `WorldState.from_canonical` are private. Ticking one with proposals leaves
  132    the other's state digest unchanged. Editing any dict or list from one's
  133    `canonical()` changes neither. Their `balances`, `sources` and
  134    `reservations` mappings are distinct objects. The restore-from-file helper
  135    lives in stream/.
  136 
  137 9. **Ownership and compatibility.** kernel/ imports nothing from stream/ or
  138    world/; stream/ imports nothing from world/; world replay and recovery live
  139    in world/ and use stream/'s file functions. The dependency-direction test
  140    is extended to check both. Nothing new imports automation/ or tests/.
  141    `viewer/world_view.py` adds v3.stream.3 to its recognised formats and
  142    nothing else, so sealed world runs still open in the OD-012 world view.
  143 
  144 10. **Runs.** Focused tests: the seal chain and tamper cases; inputs equal the
  145     submitted proposals, including empty ticks and the boolean case;
  146     `from_canonical` round trips over the 1a/1b fixture states and every tick
  147     of a 200-tick scenario run, with malformed input refused; replay identical
  148     for scenario runs at seeds 7, 11 and 23 over 120 ticks, and non-identical
  149     at exactly the edited tick when one input or one recorded outcome is
  150     altered; recovery on a truncated line, on a broken last seal, and on a cut
  151     at the first tick at or after tick 40 with at least one live reservation;
  152     isolation as in item 8. Optional world targets: replay identical at seeds
  153     7, 11 and 23 over 120 ticks with yield on/off and scoring on/off, one
  154     altered decision caught at its tick, and one world recovery. Frozen
  155     references under evidence/stage-01/slice-1c/ with SHA256SUMS.txt: the
  156     scenario, seed 7, 120 ticks (expected about 0.66 MB), and the default
  157     world configuration, seed 7, 120 ticks (expected about 0.31 MB, optional
  158     target). Their file sha256 and final seals go in the results.
  159 
  160 11. **Reference results.** The committed 1b fixture output
  161     `evidence/stage-01/slice-1b/fixtures-stage1b.txt` is the before-capture:
  162     its state and record digests are sha256 over kernel canonical bytes, and
  163     `fixture_digests.py` reproduced it exactly at `6f50040` before this
  164     declaration. After the slice, that output is unchanged and
  165     `tests/fixtures/head-b305783-seed7-ticks80-decisions.json` still matches.
  166     Nothing is regenerated.
  167 
  168 12. **Stop and halt.** The slice closes when items 1–11 hold and a results
  169     entry below this declaration records: what was built; the references'
  170     sha256 and final seals; replay results per seed; the recovery cut tick and
  171     the live reservations at the cut; the reference results; and reproduction
  172     commands for the independent reviewer. Halt and report if any step needs a
  173     settlement or decision change, a kernel or schema version change, a new
  174     lever, a change to a recorded fixture or reference digest, or a kernel
  175     canonical-form change; if replay diverges for any reason other than a
  176     defect in new 1c code; or if the suite fails for a reason outside this
  177     slice.
  178 
  179 13. **Commits.** A: items 1–6 and 9 with their tests. B: items 7 and 8, the
  180     frozen references and the results entry. Each commit: the whole suite
  181     green in one invocation, an ASCII subject, the message from a file, local
  182     only, no push.
  183 
  184 Changes from the 2026-09-23 build brief, decided here: no git fields in the
  185 file, because they would enter every seal; `params_typed` for params without a
  186 canonical form; recovery restores sealed content identical to the
  187 uninterrupted run instead of writing a `resumed_from` header; the scenario
  188 generator is rebuilt and checked on recovery; world replay and recovery live
  189 in world/, so stream/ still does not import world/; the viewer recognises the
  190 new format; sizes are measured estimates (about 0.66 MB and 0.31 MB), not
  191 "well under 300 KB"; world checks and the world reference are optional
  192 targets, because Stage 1 is kernel-only.

  341   22% and its inputs about 8%. A world file averages about 2.6 KB per tick.
  342   ROADMAP's ceiling at 50 actors is an average of at most 64 KiB per tick of
  343   replay plus decision evidence; slice 1d measures it.

  345   (world), not the write. Their means over 120 ticks on the owner's machine

  591 **Built.** `viewer/world_view.py`: generator (`py -3 -B -m viewer.world_view
  592 runs/<run>.jsonl`, writes `runs/<run>.world.html`), a pure JS core
  593 (`WorldViewCore`: parse lines, build typed arrays, compute one display frame)
  594 and the page script (canvas 2D, 2:1 isometric tiles, depth-sorted sprites,
  595 requestAnimationFrame playback with ease-in-out tween of position and hunger
  596 colour, speed 1–20 t/s, integer scrubber, step, pan/zoom/fit, click-to-inspect
  597 person or source, legend, footer). The page embeds the run file's complete
  598 text as one JSON string (every less-than sign written as its JSON unicode
  599 escape, so no byte sequence in the data can close the script element) and
  600 carries the file's
  601 SHA-256 in `<meta name="source-sha256">`. The generator recognises exactly
  602 the header shape in the card and refuses anything else by name. Rule 4 as
  603 implemented: at progress 0 the frame equals the recorded state (asserted in
  604 `node` at ticks 0, 1, 777 and n on the synthetic fixture); the inspect panel
  605 reads recorded positions, hunger, band label, food, trait, decision line,
  606 scores and kernel outcomes for state/tick `t`, and re-renders on tick change
  607 during playback, never on the tween; a person whose `died_at` is `k` stops at
  608 boundary `k` and their marker fades over three ticks; yield rings follow the
  609 recorded decision of tick `t`.

  690 ### Stage 1b declarations — fixed before implementation
  691 
  692 - `reserve` wraps one existing `claim`, `transfer` or `consume` operation. The
  693   existing shape, authority, balanced-effect and affordability checks apply to
  694   its whole frozen plan. Nested reservations and arbitrary effects are excluded.
  695 - A hold encumbers the plan's debit accounts without moving their stock.
  696   Balances/source stocks include held units; the conserved total counts each
  697   unit once. Free availability is tick-start stock minus all existing holds.
  698   Accepting a new reservation immediately reduces remaining free availability.
  699 - Settlement assigns each accepted reservation an action ID from the canonical
  700   digest of its creation tick, actor and engine-assigned sequence. Callers use
  701   the returned ID for completion/cancellation. IDs cannot be reused at later
  702   ticks. There is no unbounded collection of completed-action tombstones.
  703 - A reservation created at T is first eligible for completion/cancellation at
  704   T+1. It remains held until its owner explicitly completes or cancels it;
  705   empty ticks do not invent an action or timeout. The sole cancellation cause
  706   in this slice is the owner's explicit request. No death/interrupt scheduler.
  707 - Validate every submitted identity/order and authority first. Then process
  708   cancellations, completions, and new transactions/reservations in that order.
  709   Within each phase use rotated actor rank and dense actor-local sequence.
  710   Malformed/unauthorised cancellations cannot suppress a valid completion.
  711 - Completion commits exactly the frozen balanced effects against its own hold,
  712   never against ordinary free availability. Cancellation removes only its hold.
  713   Neither returns debit availability to ordinary proposals during that tick.
  714   Completion credits and released stock are spendable at the next tick start.
  715 - Duplicate proposal IDs/orders retain the existing deny-all rule. Distinct
  716   terminal requests for one action resolve in phase/sequence order; after the
  717   first valid close, further requests are denied as `denied_unknown_action`.
  718   A rejected unrelated transaction cannot cancel a reservation. New holds
  719   cannot be completed in their creation tick, even with a guessed action ID.
  720 - State/views include immutable reservations. Accepted reserve outcomes expose
  721   the frozen plan and ID; terminal outcomes carry its ID and committed effects
  722   (none for cancellation). Every submission retains an explicit outcome.
  723 - The new schema adds reservation state and action fields. Legacy no-action
  724   balances, effects, reasons and priority must remain unchanged. Canonical
  725   digests intentionally change with the schema; historical references remain
  726   intact and no new accepted baseline is inferred from self-verification.
  727 
  728 ### Stage 1b result — 2026-09-21
  729 
  730 Implemented in the main simulation checkout at `C:/dev/03-Living-World-V3`,
  731 as uncommitted changes on `codex/kernel-first-slice` based on `b915aa0`.
  732 There is no new isolated implementation checkout to synchronize. Existing
  733 dirty automation files, historical evidence and receipts were preserved.
  734 
  735 `kernel/state.py` owns immutable reservations and free-stock observation;
  736 `proposals.py` supplies reserve/complete/cancel requests; `settlement.py` owns
  737 validation, phased resolution, hold acquisition/release and atomic effects.
  738 `outcomes.py` records the generated action ID and frozen plan at reservation,
  739 and the target ID and effects on closure. Ordinary account totals include held
  740 units once. No action history accumulates after closure.
